recovered · docs/research/01-market-landscape.md · 9.7 KB
# 01: Market landscape: moving AI work between tools

*Researched 2026-10-11. Sources are linked inline. Vendor pages are marketing claims unless noted, and
figures should be re-checked before external use.*

## 1. The problem in one line

People do real work inside AI chats: plans, research, code, files, decisions. That work is stuck in
the tool where it happened. Moving it to another assistant, a coding agent, a teammate or a business
system is **manual, lossy and expert-only**.

This session showed it directly. A public ChatGPT share link returned *only its title* to a
standard web fetch. Getting the content out meant reverse-engineering an undocumented stream format. Even
then the source had **redacted every tool output** and kept none of the generated files (see
[06-loss-audit-findings.md](06-loss-audit-findings.md)).

## 2. Who is already here

### 2a. Platform-native "switching" features (2026)

| Player | What moves | What does not | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Claude "Import memory"** (Mar 2026) | A memory summary produced by running a Claude prompt inside the old assistant | Full conversations, files, tool runs. Memories take about 24 h to appear. | [PCWorld](https://pcworld.com/article/3076376/claude-can-now-import-chat-histories-from-chatgpt-and-other-ais.html), [Tom's Guide](https://www.tomsguide.com/ai/you-can-move-your-chatgpt-memory-to-claude-in-60-seconds-heres-how), [Blog du Modérateur](https://www.blogdumoderateur.com/anthropic-facilite-migration-chatgpt-claude/) |
| **Gemini "Import memory / Import chats"** (late Mar 2026) | Memory via prompt, plus chat-history ZIPs from ChatGPT or Claude exports (5 ZIPs/day, 5 GB each) | Not in the UK, Switzerland or EEA. **Uploaded files and images do not transfer.** | [gHacks](https://www.ghacks.net/2026/03/31/google-adds-chatgpt-and-claude-import-tools-to-gemini-for-memory-and-chat-history/), [WinBuzzer](https://winbuzzer.com/2026/03/27/google-gemini-imports-chats-memory-chatgpt-claude-xcxwbn/), [Android Headlines](https://www.androidheadlines.com/2026/03/google-gemini-import-chat-history-memory-how-to.html) |
| **Claude Code `--teleport`** | Claude Code *web* sessions moved to the local CLI | Ordinary claude.ai chats. Moving a chat into Claude Code was requested as a feature ("teleport from chat"). | [claudelog](https://claudelog.com/faqs/what-is-teleport-in-claude-code/), [feature request](https://claudeissues.com/issue/23017-feature-transfer-chat-context-to-claude-code-session-teleport-from-chat) |
| **Official data exports** (ChatGPT, Claude) | Account-wide ZIP with `conversations.json` | Undocumented schema. Claude's export excludes memory, and the download link expires after 24 h. | [OpenAI community](https://community.openai.com/t/decoding-exported-data-by-parsing-conversations-json-and-or-chat-html/403144), [saveai.net](https://saveai.net/blog/how-to-export-claude-conversations) |

**Read:** the labs now compete to *receive* switchers. The feature they ship moves **memory**, a profile
of the user. It does not move **work state**, meaning what was being built, what was decided, what code
ran and which files exist.

### 2b. Memory-portability start-ups and open source

| Player | Positioning | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Egoist Machines **AI Passport** | Consumer context layer, approve-to-share | YC-backed, **$3M seed**, launched 2026-10-07 ([GlobeNewswire via Manila Times](https://www.manilatimes.net/2026/10/07/tmt-newswire/globenewswire/egoist-machines-ai-passport-goes-live-today-only-way-to-make-your-personal-context-portable-across-ai-assistants-like-chatgpt-claude-muse-and-gemini-but-still-owned-by-you/2440954)) |
| **Mem0 / OpenMemory** extension | "Universal, portable memory" across ChatGPT, Claude, Perplexity, Grok and Gemini | [mem0.ai](https://mem0.ai/blog/introducing-the-openmemory-chrome-extension) |
| **Walrus Memory** | Portable agent memory (June 2026) | [TechEdgeAI](https://techedgeai.com/walrus-unveils-portable-walrus-memory-for-ai-agents-integrating-claude-chatgpt-gemini-and-openclaw-nemoclaw/) |
| **Context Pack**, Echo, MemoryLake, memx | Memory packs and cross-model memory | [context-pack.com](https://www.context-pack.com/), [echoknows](https://echoknows.com/cross-platform-ai-memory), [Plurality roundup](https://plurality.network/blogs/best-universal-ai-memory-extensions-2026/) |
| **context-use** (OSS CLI), **portable-ai-memory** (OSS) | Turn exports into memory | [onfabric](https://onfabric.substack.com/p/context-use-turn-your-data-exports), [GitHub](https://github.com/chandanpandeys/portable-ai-memory) |

### 2c. Exporters (browser extensions)

AI Chat Exporter (Backrun; freemium, about 7 full exports per month free), AI Exporter (SaveAI), LLM Chat Exporter,
ai.md (OSS, YAML front-matter) and Chatport (MD, PDF, DOCX, JSON, CSV). Sources: [Substack comparison](https://myzservices.substack.com/p/ai-chat-export-tools-ai-exporter),
[AlternativeTo](https://alternativeto.net/software/ai-chat-exporter-extension/), [ai.md](https://addons.mozilla.org/firefox/addon/ai-md/),
[Chatport](https://addons.mozilla.org/en-US/firefox/addon/chatport/), [Backrun](https://backrun.co/blog/ai-chat-exporter-chrome-extension).

**Read:** these produce *documents for humans*. None produces a *continuation package for an agent*. None
audits what the source dropped, and none recovers files.

### 2d. Coding-session history

**SpecStory** saves Cursor, Copilot, Claude Code and Codex sessions as local Markdown. Capture is free;
Cloud Pro is $25/mo and Cloud Team $300/mo ([pricing](https://specstory.com/pricing), [docs](https://docs.specstory.com/specstory/quickstart)).
It is the closest analogue for *IDE agents*. It does not cover consumer chat apps or cross-vendor handoff.

### 2e. Enterprise capture and compliance

**Smarsh** captures ChatGPT Enterprise and Microsoft 365 Copilot prompts, responses and files as records
for SEC 17a-4 / FINRA obligations ([ChatGPT Enterprise](https://www.smarsh.com/channel/chatgpt-enterprise), [Copilot](https://www.smarsh.com/channel/microsoft-365-copliot/)).
Capture of this kind exists for regulated finance, but it is archive-only. It does not *hand off* work.

### 2f. Agent interoperability standards (what we should plug into, not compete with)

- **MCP**: the standard tool and context protocol for agents.
- **Agent Skills / `SKILL.md`**: open standard published by Anthropic (Dec 2025). Reported adopters include
  Codex, Cursor, Copilot and Gemini CLI ([DenserAI](https://denser.ai/blog/agent-skills-guide/), [mcp.directory](https://mcp.directory/blog/cross-agent-skills-cursor-codex-cline-antigravity-gemini-mastra-portability), [spec](https://github.com/agentskills/agentskills)).
- **A2A** (Linux Foundation): more than 150 organisations, in production across major clouds ([LF press](https://linuxfoundation.org/press/a2a-protocol-surpasses-150-organizations-lands-in-major-cloud-platforms-and-sees-enterprise-production-use-in-first-year)).
  It is agent-to-agent *runtime* messaging, not *persisted work handoff*.

## 3. The gap, stated precisely

| Need | Memory tools | Exporters | Platform import | SpecStory | Smarsh | **Baton (this build)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Move user preferences | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ➖ (not the goal) |
| Full transcript incl. **reasoning** + **tool calls** | ❌ | partial | partial | ✅ (IDE only) | ✅ (archive) | ✅ |
| **Agent-ready continuation brief** (goal, state, next steps) | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ `HANDOFF.md` |
| **Loss audit** (what the source dropped) | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **File recovery** from executed code | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ (files explicitly dropped) | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Works from a **public share link**, no login | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Lands in **Claude Code / Codex / Cursor** as a Skill | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Lands in **Salesforce / Jira / Zendesk / Slack** | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | archive only | ✅ |
| Tamper-evident integrity | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ (hash chain) |
| Local-first, free | varies | freemium | ✅ | ✅ capture | ❌ | ✅ |

## 4. Demand signals

- Every major lab shipped switching features within ~30 days of each other (Mar 2026). That is a strong signal
  that **cross-tool movement is a growth lever for the receiver**, which supports a "destination pays" model
  (see [04-monetization.md](04-monetization.md)).
- Shadow AI is widespread: Gartner (Q1 2026) reports 88% of employees with an employer tool also use a personal
  one for work. Verizon DBIR 2026 puts unsanctioned personal access at 67% of AI users ([Deel summary](https://www.deel.com/deel-works/shadow-ai-workplace),
  [CloudEagle on Netskope](https://www.cloudeagle.ai/blogs/the-shadow-ai-economy)). Work done in personal chats is
  **institutional knowledge that never reaches the system of record**. That is the business-layer wedge.
- Policy pressure: the Data Transfer Initiative calls AI assistants a "high priority" portability area and piloted an
  export format with Inflection AI. CDT notes a joint lab standard is unlikely without user and policy pressure ([CDT](https://cdt.org/insights/dont-let-perfect-be-the-enemy-of-portable/),
  [DTI DMA response](https://dtinit.org/assets/DTI-Response-to-DMA-One-Year-Review.pdf)). An **open, neutral format** fills that gap.

## 5. Implication for the build

Do not build "another memory app" or "another exporter". Build the **work-handoff layer**:

1. A neutral, open **bundle format** (transcript + reasoning + tools + files + loss audit + integrity).
2. **Ingest** from where work happens: share links, official exports, coding-agent logs.
3. **Emit** to where work continues: coding agents (Skill, AGENTS.md), people (HTML, MD), and systems of record
   (CRM, ITSM, ticketing, chat).
4. Be **free and local-first** for individuals; monetise destinations and enterprises.
recovered · docs/research/02-source-formats.md · 5.8 KB
# 02: Source formats: where AI work lives and how to read it

*Findings from hands-on reverse-engineering in this session, plus community documentation. None of these
formats is officially documented by its vendor. Parsers must stay defensive and keep the raw source.*

## A. ChatGPT public share link (`https://chatgpt.com/share/<uuid>`)

**Discovery path (this session):**

1. A plain fetch with an HTML-to-text tool returned only `<title>`. The body is rendered on the client.
2. A raw `curl` with a browser User-Agent returned HTTP 200 and 1.4 MB of HTML.
3. The data sits inside `<script>` tags as
   `window.__reactRouterContext.streamController.enqueue("<JSON-string>")`.
   Two chunks appeared: a 731,900-character payload and a 12-character terminator.
4. The payload is a **React Router turbo-stream** (a flattened, de-duplicated graph):
   - The top level is a JSON array.
   - Objects are encoded as `{"_<keyIndex>": <valueIndex>, ...}`. Keys and values are **indices into the same
     array**, which de-duplicates repeated strings.
   - Negative integers are sentinels (`-5` = undefined, `-7` = null, and so on).
   - Arrays hold value indices. A few special arrays start with a tag such as `"D"` (Date) or `"P"` (Promise).
5. After decoding, `loaderData["routes/share.$shareId.($action)"].serverResponse.data` has the same shape as
   one conversation in the official export: `title`, `create_time`, `mapping{}`, `current_node`, `model`,
   `safe_urls[]` and more.

**Message tree:** `mapping[id] = {id, parent, children[], message}`. Walking `current_node → parent → … → root`
and reversing gives the visible branch. In this fixture, 406 nodes formed one linear chain.

**`content.content_type` values seen:**

| content_type | role | Meaning | Text location |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | user / assistant / tool / system | Prose | `parts[]` (strings) |
| `thoughts` | assistant | **Reasoning summaries** | `thoughts[] {summary, content}` |
| `reasoning_recap` | assistant | "Worked for 24m 59s" | `content` |
| `code` | assistant → `container.exec` | **Executed shell or Python** | `text` |
| `model_editable_context` | assistant (hidden) | Memory snapshot | `model_set_context` (empty here) |

**Rich rendering:** assistant text can contain GenUI JSX (`<box>`, `<grid>`, `<Cite refs={…}/>`,
`<AsyncImage/>`). A clean Markdown fallback is stored at `metadata.model_dil_v2.fallbackMarkdown`, with
private-use-area entity markers: `U+E200 name U+E202 arg … U+E201` (for example `cite`, `image_group`).

**Tool routing:** `recipient` on assistant messages: `web.run`, `container.exec`, `functions.exec`,
`api_tool.call_tool` (connectors such as GitHub, Canva and Hugging Face) and opaque ids such as `q7dr546` (memory).
`metadata.reasoning_title(s)` carries a human label such as *"Searching OpenAlex…"*.

**Redaction in public shares:** every tool output in the fixture reads `"The output of this plugin was redacted."`
This held for all 52 container execs, 52 API-tool calls, 33 `functions.exec`, 20 image opens and 6 web runs.
`functions.exec` *inputs* were also blank. Details are in [06-loss-audit-findings.md](06-loss-audit-findings.md).

## B. ChatGPT official data export (`conversations.json` in the account ZIP)

A JSON **array** of conversation objects with the same `mapping` / `current_node` shape as A
([community](https://community.openai.com/t/decoding-exported-data-by-parsing-conversations-json-and-or-chat-html/403144),
[levelup](https://levelup.gitconnected.com/your-chatgpt-history-is-a-tree-not-a-transcript-2026-5660ceb301d4)).
Edits and regenerations create **branches**. Discarded branches stay in `mapping`. Open WebUI detects the
format by the presence of a `mapping` key ([Open WebUI docs](https://docs.openwebui.com/features/chat-conversations/data-controls/import-export)).
**Decision:** A and B share one normaliser.

## C. Claude.ai official data export (`conversations.json`)

An array of `{uuid, name, created_at, updated_at, chat_messages[]}`. Each message has `sender` (`human` or `assistant`),
`text`, `created_at`, and in newer exports a `content[]` array of typed blocks (`text`, `thinking`, `tool_use`,
`tool_result`). Attachments appear as `attachments[]` (with `extracted_content`) and `files[]`.
Memory is **not** included ([saveai.net](https://saveai.net/blog/how-to-export-claude-conversations)).
**Decision:** prefer `content[]` blocks when present and fall back to `text`.

## D. Claude Code session log (`~/.claude/projects/<cwd-slug>/<session-id>.jsonl`)

One JSON object per line. Records with `type: "user" | "assistant"` carry `message.content`, which is either a string or an
array of blocks (`text`, `thinking`, `tool_use{name,input}`, `tool_result{tool_use_id, content}`, `image`).
Other record types (`summary`, `system`, attachments, file-history snapshots) carry metadata.
`isSidechain` marks sub-agent traffic. `cwd`, `gitBranch`, `version` and `timestamp` are present on most lines.
**Decision:** this is the dogfood source, because Baton exports the session that built Baton.

## E. Rejected or deferred sources (v1)

| Source | Reason |
|---|---|
| Gemini (Takeout "My Activity" HTML) | Activity log, not threaded conversations. Needs HTML scraping. Deferred. |
| Logged-in ChatGPT `backend-api/conversation/{id}` | Needs the user's session token. That belongs in a browser extension running in the user's own session, not a server. Roadmap. |
| Cursor / Copilot / Codex local logs | SpecStory covers these. The generic Markdown and JSONL parser handles them for now. Roadmap. |

## F. Design consequences

1. **Keep the raw input** inside the bundle (`source/`), so better parsers can re-run later without loss.
2. **Normalise to events, not just messages.** One turn holds prose, reasoning, tool calls and tool results.
3. **Record provenance per field** (`redacted_by_source`, `recovered_from`), so the loss audit is computed, not guessed.
recovered · docs/research/03-legal-and-policy.md · 4.3 KB
# 03: Legal, policy and trust considerations

*Not legal advice. This doc records the design constraints the build follows and why.*

## 1. Is it legitimate to read a share link?

- A share link is **published by its owner** to anyone with the URL. Baton fetches **one URL the user supplies**,
  the same way a browser would. It does not crawl, enumerate share IDs, bypass authentication or defeat
  rate limits. **Design rule: one user-supplied URL per request, no discovery and no bulk scraping.**
- The web app also works **without fetching at all**. The user saves the page ("Save as…") or downloads the
  official export, and Baton parses it **in the browser**. Nothing leaves the device.
- The user's own data exports are theirs to use. Baton only reads what the user provides.

## 2. Data-portability law (why an open format matters)

- **GDPR Art. 20** gives a right to receive personal data *provided by* the data subject in a "structured,
  commonly used and machine-readable format", and Recital 68 adds "interoperable" ([Art. 20 text](https://advisera.com/gdpr/right-to-data-portability/),
  [Recital 68](https://presencis.com/regulations/gdpr/recital-68/)). Prompts are clearly in scope. Whether *model outputs*
  are "provided by" the user is **unsettled** ([analysis](https://www.iiprd.com/?p=14136)).
- **DTI** calls AI assistants a priority portability area and piloted an AI export format with Inflection AI
  ([DTI DMA response](https://dtinit.org/assets/DTI-Response-to-DMA-One-Year-Review.pdf),
  [CDT](https://cdt.org/insights/dont-let-perfect-be-the-enemy-of-portable/)).
  **Opportunity:** publish the Baton bundle schema as an open spec and offer it to DTI as a candidate interoperable format.

## 3. Records, audit and retention (business layer)

- **EU AI Act Art. 12 / 19 / 26(6):** automatic event logging for high-risk systems, with deployer log retention of at least
  six months. Commentators place the application date at 2 Aug 2026 and stress integrity against silent alteration
  ([Help Net Security](https://www.helpnetsecurity.com/2026/04/16/eu-ai-act-logging-requirements/),
  [FireTail via Security Boulevard](https://securityboulevard.com/2026/04/article-12-and-the-logging-mandate-what-the-eu-ai-act-actually-requires-firetail-blog/)).
  Verify the date against EUR-Lex, because the digital-omnibus proposals may shift it.
- **SEC 17a-4 / FINRA:** Smarsh markets ChatGPT Enterprise and Copilot capture for these obligations
  ([Smarsh](https://www.smarsh.com/channel/chatgpt-enterprise)).
- **E-discovery:** AI chat logs are discoverable. In *NYT v. OpenAI* the court ordered preservation (May 2025),
  modified it (Oct 2025) and ordered production of 20M anonymised logs (Nov 2025)
  ([Duane Morris](https://blogs.duanemorris.com/classactiondefense/2025/10/27/new-york-federal-courts-openai-discovery-orders-provide-key-insights-for-companies-navigating-ai-preservation-standards/),
  [terms.law](https://terms.law/2025/11/12/openai-v-new-york-times-stopped-being-just-a-copyright-case-the-moment-the-court-turned-to-your-chatgpt-logs/)).

**Design consequence:** each bundle carries a **SHA-256 hash chain** over its normalised events plus a root
hash, so any later edit is detectable. This gives *tamper-evidence*, not *tamper-proofing*. Proofing would need
WORM storage or external anchoring, which belongs to the enterprise tier.

## 4. Privacy defaults

| Default | Why |
|---|---|
| **Redaction on by default** for emails, phone numbers, API keys and tokens (`sk-…`, `ghp_…`, AWS keys, JWTs, bearer tokens), and private keys | Handoffs get pasted into other tools and repos. Secrets in AI chats are common. |
| Hidden or system messages **excluded** unless `--include-hidden` | They can contain memory or profile data the user never saw |
| Local-first: CLI, Skill and MCP all run on the user's machine. The web app parses in the browser. | No server copy, so no breach surface and no lawful-access target |
| Generated replay scripts are **never executed automatically** | They contain third-party code from another AI's sandbox |

## 5. Trademarks

Vendor names (ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Salesforce, Jira, Zendesk, Slack) appear only to name *formats and
integrations*. There is no logos-as-branding and no implied endorsement. The product name **Baton** is generic. A clash
check before any commercial launch is listed in the roadmap.
recovered · docs/research/04-monetization.md · 4.3 KB
# 04: Monetisation: free to the user, paid by whoever gains

Principle: **the person moving their work never pays.** Revenue comes from the parties who *gain* when
work moves: the receiving AI vendor, the employer whose system of record fills up, the platform that wants an
"Import" button. Data is never sold. That is a hard rule and a trust moat.

## Models, ranked by fit

| # | Model | Who pays | Mechanism | Why it fits | Risks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Destination-pays switching bounty** | AI vendors | Verified-migration API: the vendor pays $X per user who lands a Baton bundle in their product. The bundle includes a signed receipt (`bundle.root_hash` + destination ack). | Labs shipped import features within ~30 days of each other in Mar 2026. That is acquisition spend already. It mirrors telecom number portability, where the receiving side funds the move. | Neutrality perception. Mitigate by paying out to *every* destination equally and publishing the rate card. |
| 2 | **Enterprise "AI Work Record" layer** | Employers (IT, compliance, legal) | Per-seat or per-record SaaS: capture → redact → hash-chain → sink to Salesforce, ServiceNow, Jira or Zendesk, with retention policies and legal hold | Shadow AI is pervasive (Gartner: 88% use personal tools too). EU AI Act logging, SEC 17a-4, e-discovery. | Long sales cycles. Smarsh-style incumbents. Start mid-market. |
| 3 | **"Import from anywhere" widget / API** (white-label) | Platforms: agent builders, IDEs, note apps | Usage-based API (per bundle parsed) plus embeddable drop zone | Every AI product wants an "import your ChatGPT project" button and none wants to maintain parsers | Commoditisation if labs standardise. The open format makes Baton the reference implementation. |
| 4 | **Teams workspace** | Small teams and agencies | $8/user/mo: shared handoff library, review links, SSO, retention | Agencies and consultants hand AI work to clients and colleagues daily | Feature creep toward docs tools |
| 5 | **Education: AI-use disclosure receipts** | Universities | Institution licence. Students attach a tamper-evident Baton bundle as an AI-use appendix. | Policies increasingly ask for AI-use records and full logs (Princeton course-level; Newcastle appendices) ([QS Insights](https://magazine.qs.com/qs-insights-magazine-31/disclosure-or-disarray), [Trinka](https://www.trinka.ai/blog/which-universities-have-the-strictest-ai-disclosure-requirements-in-2026/)) | Must avoid surveillance framing. Student-controlled sharing only. |
| 6 | **Proof-of-work portfolios** | Freelancers (freemium upsell) | Public handoff pages showing *how* a deliverable was produced, with custom domains | Clients increasingly ask "how did you use AI?" | Small ARPU |
| 7 | **Template marketplace** | Creators (rev-share) | Handoff templates such as "PRD handoff", "research → paper", "support escalation" | Network effects | Needs scale first |
| 8 | **Sponsorships / grants** | Foundations, DTI-aligned funders | Fund the open spec and reference implementation | Public-interest portability | Not a business on its own |

**Explicitly rejected:** selling or "anonymised" licensing of conversation data, ads inside handoffs, and paywalling
export of the user's own data. Each one breaks the trust the product depends on.

## Unit sketch (illustrative, not a forecast)

- Consumer CLI, Skill and web app: about $0 marginal cost, because parsing is local or in the browser and there is no LLM call by default.
  Summarisation is done by *the destination agent* the user already pays for (see ADR-001).
- Switching bounty: if a vendor's paid-plan CAC is in the tens of dollars, a **$1–5 verified-migration fee**
  is cheap for them and sustainable for Baton at low volume.
- Enterprise: priced against compliance-archive comparables, per seat per month, with sinks as add-ons.

## Creative "free to user" mechanics

- **"Carry it forward" receipts:** each import gives the user a portable proof of what they moved. No lock-in to Baton either:
  the bundle is plain files.
- **Open spec plus certification:** vendors pay to be *"Baton-Ready"* certified, meaning they import and export bundles losslessly.
  This mirrors standards-body economics.
- **Bring-your-own-agent summarisation:** heavy LLM work runs on the user's existing subscription inside Claude Code,
  Codex or Cursor via the Skill, so Baton never needs an inference budget for free users.
recovered · docs/research/05-business-layer.md · 3.6 KB
# 05: The same problem in business software, and Baton as a layer

## The business version of "I can't move my ChatGPT work"

| Scenario | Today | With the Baton layer |
|---|---|---|
| **AI agent → human escalation** (support) | Agentforce passes session ID, contact, escalation reason and a summary into Omni-Channel ([Salesforce](https://www.salesforce.com/blog/agent-to-human-handoff/), [Towards AI](https://pub.towardsai.net/one-brain-every-touchpoint-building-adaptive-multi-channel-agentforce-architectures-d3c16af9b4c7)). This only works *inside* Salesforce's own agent. | Any bot (Intercom Fin, a custom GPT, a Claude agent) emits a Baton bundle. The sink writes a **Case + Task + transcript note** with the loss audit and next steps attached. |
| **Rep researches an account in a personal ChatGPT** | Insights stay in the chat, where the CRM never sees them (shadow AI) | Rep pastes the share link into a Slack command or browser button. A redacted handoff is attached to the **Opportunity**. |
| **Engineer designs in a chat, implements in an IDE agent** | Copy-paste and lost context. "Teleport from chat" is a requested Claude Code feature. | `baton import <link>` → `HANDOFF.md` + Skill. The IDE agent continues with recovered files. |
| **Incident triage in AI chat → ITSM** | A manual summary in the ticket | A **Jira / ServiceNow issue** created with timeline, commands run (from tool calls) and an integrity hash |
| **Compliance / legal hold** | Archive-only capture (Smarsh) in regulated finance | A hash-chained bundle per conversation, retention-tagged, exportable to the archive or e-discovery |
| **Consultant → client delivery** | A PDF report. The *method* is lost. | A shareable HTML handoff with prompts, reasoning and artifacts (proof of work) |

## Architecture of the layer

```
 sources                          core (open, zero-dep)                     sinks
 ─────────                        ──────────────────────                    ─────
 ChatGPT share / export  ──┐      parse → normalise (events)                Markdown / HTML / ZIP
 Claude export           ──┤──▶   redact (PII, secrets)          ──▶        Agent Skill / AGENTS.md
 Claude Code JSONL       ──┤      recover files from exec code              MCP tools (any agent)
 generic MD / JSON       ──┘      loss audit + SHA-256 hash chain           Salesforce · Jira · Zendesk · Slack
```

**Sinks are payload builders, not hard dependencies.** v1 emits API-ready JSON for each system:
Salesforce composite request, Jira REST v3 issue with ADF body, Zendesk ticket and Slack Block Kit. They can be
POSTed by the CLI (`--post` with env credentials) or by the customer's own middleware (MuleSoft, Workato, Zapier).
This keeps the open core free of vendor SDKs and lets enterprises keep credentials in their own systems.

## Why a "layer" and not a CRM app

- The value is **cross-system**: one normalised record feeds CRM, ITSM, chat and archive.
- Lock-in risk is reversed. Baton is the neutral switch, so customers can change AI vendors *or* CRMs.
- It mirrors how Segment became the layer between event sources and destinations: **sources × destinations** is a
  quadratic integration problem that a canonical format reduces to linear.

## Enterprise tier, beyond v1 (roadmap)

- Org policy packs: redaction rules, retention, legal hold and destination allow-lists
- WORM storage or external anchoring of root hashes (tamper-*proof*, not only tamper-evident)
- SSO/SCIM, audit dashboards, DLP integrations
- Managed connectors with OAuth (Salesforce Connected App, Atlassian OAuth 2.0, Zendesk OAuth)
recovered · docs/research/06-loss-audit-findings.md · 2.6 KB
# 06: Loss audit findings: what a ChatGPT share link silently drops

*Measured on the real fixture `fixtures/chatgpt-share-cricket-stumping.html.gz`
(share `6acbc222-2500-83ee-b38c-dcfe2b46e388`, "Cricket Stumping Simulation", 2026-10-11).*

## Counts (from decoding the share payload)

| Item | Present in share | Lost / redacted |
|---|---|---|
| User prompts | **8 / 8** | none |
| Final assistant replies | 14 visible + 39 hidden "commentary" | none, but rich UI is flattened |
| Reasoning summaries (`thoughts`) | **46 blocks** | Full chain of thought was never exposed. Summaries only. |
| Executed code: `container.exec` inputs | **52 / 52** (120,715 chars) | none |
| Executed code: `functions.exec` inputs | 0 / 33 | **33 blank**. This is the code that built `app.py` and `stumping_model.py`. |
| Tool outputs, all tools | 0 / 163+ | **all redacted**: "The output of this plugin was redacted." |
| Web search results / citations | Queries only (`reasoning_titles`) | Result pages and `turn…searchN` targets are gone. `safe_urls[]` keeps a flat URL list. |
| Generated files (`sandbox:/mnt/data/...`) | Links in text | **Not downloadable from the share.** Every file link is dead for the recipient. |
| Canva design, GitHub actions | Links only | The connector outputs are redacted |

## Recoverability

Files written with heredocs inside `container.exec` (`cat > path <<'EOF' … EOF`) can be **reconstructed byte for
byte** from the code text. In this fixture that covers `simulate_stumping.py` (two versions), `build_paper.py`, `paper.md`,
`make_paper.py`, `references.bib`, `README.md`, `requirements.txt`, `LICENSE`, `DATA_AND_VALIDATION.md`,
`Dockerfile`, `.gitignore`, `CITATION.cff`, `.github/workflows/tests.yml`, `README.txt` and `run.log`.

Files later edited in place by a Python `s.replace(...)` script are recovered **at their last heredoc version and marked
`stale`**. Files whose creating code was blanked (`functions.exec`), such as `app.py`, `stumping_model.py`, the PDF and
the DOCX, are marked **`lost`**. Baton adds a *replay script* that re-runs every recovered exec cell in order inside a
sandbox you control. That can regenerate derived binaries like the PDF.

## Why this matters for the product

- The recipient of a share link sees polished prose saying *"Download the complete ZIP"*, but the ZIP does not exist for them.
  **A transfer tool that does not say what is missing misleads by omission.** The loss audit is a core feature.
- Recovering ~15 project files from a "dead" share link is a differentiator no exporter offers. It turns a
  conversation back into a **repository**.
recovered · docs/SPEC.md · 5.7 KB
# Baton bundle format: `baton.bundle/v1`

**Status:** draft 1 (2026-10-11) · **License:** CC-BY-4.0 for this document, Apache-2.0 for the reference implementation

A Baton bundle is a vendor-neutral record of a piece of AI work: what the user asked, what the assistant
said and reasoned, which tools it ran, which files it produced, and **what the source failed to include**.
A bundle is designed to be read by three audiences at once: an AI agent continuing the work, a person reviewing it, and a
system of record filing it.

## 1. Layout

A bundle is a directory (or a ZIP of one). Only `bundle.json` is normative. Everything else is a rendering of it.

```
<slug>/
  bundle.json      REQUIRED  canonical record (this spec)
  HANDOFF.md       RECOMMENDED  agent + human brief; front matter repeats id, source and root_hash
  PROMPT.md        paste-ready single block for chat UIs
  TRANSCRIPT.md    chronological rendering
  LOSS_AUDIT.md    rendering of `loss` + `artifacts`
  index.html       offline viewer (must not load remote resources)
  SKILL.md         Agent Skills front matter (`name`, `description`) so the folder is a skill
  AGENTS.md        pointer for agents that read AGENTS.md
  files/<path>     artifact contents for status recovered | stale | attached
  replay/          optional: source code cells + a sandboxed runner
  sinks/<system>.json  optional: business-system payloads
  source/          optional: the raw input
```

## 2. `bundle.json`

```ts
interface BatonBundle {
  schema: 'baton.bundle/v1';
  id: string;                 // 16 hex chars, sha256(kind ␀ conversationId|url ␀ title ␀ firstEventText)[:16]
  title: string;
  source: {
    kind: 'chatgpt-share' | 'chatgpt-export' | 'claude-export' | 'claude-code' | 'openai-messages' | 'unknown';
    tool: string;             // human name of the source product
    url?: string; conversationId?: string; createdAt?: string; updatedAt?: string;
    model?: string; cwd?: string; gitBranch?: string;
  };
  events: BatonEvent[];       // chronological, the active branch only
  artifacts: BatonArtifact[]; // sorted by path
  loss: LossItem[];           // sorted by severity: high, warn, info
  links: string[];            // URLs referenced by messages or listed by the source
  redaction: { enabled: boolean; counts: Record<string, number> };
  integrity: { algorithm: 'sha256-chain'; root: string; count: number };
  stats: BundleStats;
  generator: { name: string; version: string; generatedAt: string };
}
```

### 2.1 Events

| `kind` | `role` | `text` | Other fields |
|---|---|---|---|
| `message` | user / assistant | Markdown | `hidden` = progress update hidden in the source UI |
| `reasoning` | assistant | reasoning text or summary | `meta.granularity` = `summary` \| `full`. `redactedBySource` when only a signature or ciphertext exists. |
| `tool_call` | assistant | short label | `tool.{name,title,language,input,args,callId}`. `redactedBySource` when the input is missing. |
| `tool_result` | tool | output | `tool.name`, `tool.callId`. `redactedBySource` when the source shows a placeholder. |
| `attachment` | user | description | `meta.{name,size,mimeType}` |
| `note` | any | structural marker | `meta.private = true` for private context (excluded by default) |

`tool.args` carries structured arguments only for file-editing tools (`Write`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `NotebookEdit`),
because artifact recovery replays them.

### 2.2 Artifacts

| `status` | Meaning | `content` |
|---|---|---|
| `recovered` | reconstructed byte for byte from the code or tool call that wrote it | present |
| `stale` | the last full write was recovered, but later modifications could not be replayed statically | present |
| `lost` | created or linked by the conversation, but its content is not in the source | absent |
| `attached` | content shipped with the source (for example, text extracted from an upload) | present |

`sha256` is the hex digest of the UTF-8 content. `recoveredFrom` / `modifiedBy` list event ids. `note` explains the status.
Paths are POSIX, relative and normalised. Absolute paths outside the source's working root are prefixed `_abs/`.
Paths never contain `..`.

### 2.3 Loss items

`{category, count, severity: 'high'|'warn'|'info', detail}`. Categories in v1: `tool-outputs`, `tool-inputs`,
`files-lost`, `files-stale`, `files-recovered`, `citations`, `images`, `user-attachments`, `branches`,
`reasoning-depth`, `memory`, `harness-context`, `subagents`, `private-context`, `baton-redactions`, `unreadable-lines`.
Producers may add categories. Consumers must display unknown ones verbatim.

## 3. Integrity

```
h0 = sha256("baton.bundle/v1")
hi = sha256(h(i-1) || stableJSON(event_i without "hash"))    for each event, in order; event_i.hash = hi
then, for each artifact sorted by path:
h  = sha256(h || stableJSON({path, status, sha256}))
root = h
```

`stableJSON` is JSON with object keys sorted lexicographically, `undefined` members dropped, and no whitespace.
A verifier recomputes artifact digests from `files/<path>` when present (otherwise from embedded `content`) and compares
the root. This gives **tamper-evidence** only. Anchoring the root externally (for example in WORM storage or a transparency log)
is out of scope for v1.

## 4. Privacy requirements for producers

1. Redaction of secrets and personal data **on by default**, with counts recorded in `redaction.counts`.
2. Private context (system prompts, memory, custom instructions, harness metadata) **excluded by default** and counted in `loss`.
3. Replay code is never executed by the producer.
4. Network access is limited to the single URL the user supplied.

## 5. Versioning

Additive fields do not change the schema id. A breaking change bumps it to `baton.bundle/v2`. Consumers should
ignore unknown fields and reject unknown major versions.

recovered · README.md · 8.7 KB
# Baton

**Hand off AI work, not just chat text.**

Baton turns an AI conversation into a **handoff bundle** that another tool, agent, teammate or business system can pick up and continue.
Sources are a ChatGPT share link, a ChatGPT or Claude data export, or a Claude Code session. Each bundle carries:

- every prompt, verbatim
- the assistant's replies and reasoning
- every tool call and the code it ran
- the **files the session created, rebuilt from that code**
- a **loss audit** of everything the source failed to hand over
- a SHA-256 hash chain so any later edit is detectable

It runs locally with zero runtime dependencies and is free to use.

```
$ npx github:nimb-ou/App import https://chatgpt.com/share/6acbc222-2500-83ee-b38c-dcfe2b46e388
✓ Cricket Stumping Simulation
  source   ChatGPT (chatgpt-share) https://chatgpt.com/share/6acbc222-2500-83ee-b38c-dcfe2b46e388
  content  8 prompts · 20 replies · 47 reasoning · 133 tool calls
  files    13 recovered · 3 stale · 16 lost
  loss     163 tool-outputs · 81 tool-inputs · 16 files-lost (see LOSS_AUDIT.md)
  root     0e489b3a871206841699f7195e270a7198f60bca19e2defbb98ffb0ec416bac6
  → handoffs/cricket-stumping-simulation/HANDOFF.md  (start here)
```

That is a real public share link. Its page *looks* complete and says "Download the complete ZIP", but the share
silently drops all 163 tool outputs, 33 code cells and every generated file. Baton reports that, rebuilds **13 project files byte
for byte** from the code that wrote them, and lists the 16 it cannot recover (including `app.py`) instead of pretending they exist.
See [`examples/cricket-stumping/`](examples/cricket-stumping/) and its [`HANDOFF.md`](examples/cricket-stumping/HANDOFF.md).

## Why

AI work is stuck where it happened. Claude and Gemini both shipped "import memory" in March 2026, but that moves a **profile
of you**, not **the work**: the plan, decisions, code and files. Exporter extensions produce documents for humans. None
of them says what was lost, and none recovers files. The full analysis is in [`docs/research/01-market-landscape.md`](docs/research/01-market-landscape.md).

## Use it the way you work

| You are… | Use | How |
|---|---|---|
| In a terminal | **CLI** | `npx github:nimb-ou/App import <url-or-file>` (Node ≥ 18) |
| In Claude Code | **Plugin** (skill + `/baton:import`, `/baton:handoff` + MCP) | `/plugin marketplace add nimb-ou/App` then `/plugin install baton@baton` |
| In any Skills-aware agent (Codex, Cursor, …) | **Agent Skill** | copy [`skills/baton/`](skills/baton/) into the agent's skills folder. The CLI is bundled inside it. |
| In an MCP client (Claude Desktop, Cursor, …) | **MCP server** | `{"command": "npx", "args": ["-y", "github:nimb-ou/App", "mcp"]}` |
| In a browser | **Web app** | open [`web/dist/baton.html`](web/dist/baton.html) (one file, works offline) and drop a file. Nothing is uploaded. |
| In a team / company | **Sinks** | `baton sink <bundle> --to salesforce\|jira\|zendesk\|slack [--post]` |

### Sources

| Input | What it is |
|---|---|
| `https://chatgpt.com/share/<id>` | public ChatGPT share link: fetched once, no login |
| `share.html` | a share page saved from the browser (works when fetching is blocked) |
| `export.zip` / `conversations.json` | ChatGPT **or** Claude account data export (`--pick "<title>"` for one conversation) |
| `*.jsonl` / a directory | Claude Code session log (`baton session latest` finds this project's newest) |
| `messages.json` | any `[{role, content}]` log (OpenAI/Anthropic API style) |

### What a bundle contains

| File | For |
|---|---|
| `HANDOFF.md` | **Start here.** Goal · every prompt verbatim · where it stopped · open threads · reasoning trail · file status · losses · how to continue |
| `PROMPT.md` | one paste-ready block for ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini or any chat that cannot read folders |
| `TRANSCRIPT.md` | everything, in order. Reasoning, tool calls and outputs are folded into `<details>`. |
| `LOSS_AUDIT.md` | what did not transfer, per-file status, integrity root |
| `files/` | rebuilt files: **recovered** (exact), **stale** (exact up to a later unreplayable edit), **lost** (listed, not faked) |
| `replay/` | the source's code cells plus a no-network Docker runner, to regenerate PDFs, ZIPs and similar outputs. Never run automatically. |
| `SKILL.md` · `AGENTS.md` | make the folder itself a "continue this work" skill or agent context |
| `index.html` | offline viewer with search |
| `bundle.json` | the canonical record: [`docs/SPEC.md`](docs/SPEC.md) |
| `sinks/*.json` | optional Salesforce / Jira / Zendesk / Slack payloads |

## CLI

```
baton import <source> [-o dir] [--zip] [--pick text] [--sink jira,slack] [--install-skill] [--no-redact] [--include-hidden]
baton session [latest|<id>]      bundle a Claude Code session from this project
baton inspect <source>           dry run: what would transfer, what would not
baton verify <bundle|zip>        recompute the hash chain (exit 2 if anything was edited)
baton sink <bundle> --to <system> [--post] [--jira-project KEY] [--sf-parent RECORD_ID]
baton mcp                        MCP server over stdio
```

Posting uses environment variables only: `SALESFORCE_INSTANCE_URL`/`SALESFORCE_ACCESS_TOKEN`,
`JIRA_BASE_URL`/`JIRA_EMAIL`/`JIRA_API_TOKEN`/`JIRA_PROJECT`, `ZENDESK_SUBDOMAIN`/`ZENDESK_EMAIL`/`ZENDESK_API_TOKEN`, `SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL`.

## Privacy and safety defaults

- **Local-first.** The CLI, skill and MCP server run on your machine. The web app parses in your browser.
- **Redaction on:** API keys (OpenAI, Anthropic, GitHub, AWS, Google, Slack, Stripe), JWTs, bearer tokens, private keys, passwords in
  assignments, emails, phone numbers and Luhn-valid card numbers. Turn off with `--no-redact`.
- **Private context off:** system prompts, memory, custom instructions and harness reminders are excluded (and counted) unless `--include-hidden` is set.
- **One URL, once:** only the public share link you pass is fetched. There is no crawling and no authentication.
- **No code execution:** replay cells are written, never run. Output paths are sanitised and writes outside the target directory are refused.

## How it is built

TypeScript with no runtime dependencies. Node ≥ 22.18 runs the source directly, and `dist/baton.mjs` is a single-file build for Node ≥ 18.

```
src/core       schema, turbo-stream decoder, SHA-256, ZIP, redaction, hash chain, build pipeline
src/parsers    ChatGPT (share + export), Claude export, Claude Code JSONL, generic logs, format detection
src/recover    file recovery (heredocs, Write/Edit replay, modification tracking) + replay kit
src/render     HANDOFF / PROMPT / TRANSCRIPT / LOSS_AUDIT / SKILL / AGENTS / index.html (safe Markdown)
src/sinks      Salesforce composite, Jira ADF, Zendesk, Slack Block Kit
src/mcp        JSON-RPC stdio server
src/node       fetch, file and zip IO, session discovery, posting
web/           client-side app (built to web/dist/baton.html)
```

```bash
npm install            # dev only: typescript, esbuild, @types/node
npm test               # 64 tests, incl. end-to-end on the real share fixture
npm run typecheck
npm run build          # dist/baton.mjs, skills/baton/scripts/baton.mjs, web/dist/*.html
npm run examples       # regenerate examples/ deterministically
```

## Business model (free to the user)

The person moving their work never pays. Revenue comes from whoever **gains** when work moves:
the receiving AI vendor (verified-migration "switching bounty"), employers (an **AI Work Record** layer that files
conversations into Salesforce, ServiceNow, Jira or Zendesk with redaction, retention and integrity), and platforms that want an "Import from anywhere"
button. Data is never sold. Details are in [`docs/research/04-monetization.md`](docs/research/04-monetization.md) and [`05-business-layer.md`](docs/research/05-business-layer.md).

## Project record

This repo documents its own making. Every prompt, decision and result is kept:

- [`docs/prompts/`](docs/prompts/): the user's prompts, verbatim, with how each was interpreted
- [`docs/journal/`](docs/journal/): timestamped build journal (what was done, bugs found, results)
- [`docs/decisions/`](docs/decisions/): architecture decision records
- [`docs/research/`](docs/research/): market, formats, legal/policy, monetisation, business layer, loss findings (with sources)
- [`examples/this-build-session/`](examples/this-build-session/): **the Claude Code session that built Baton, exported by Baton**

---
name: baton
description: Move AI work between tools without losing it. Imports a ChatGPT share link, a ChatGPT or Claude data export, or a Claude Code session log into an audited handoff bundle (HANDOFF.md, the full transcript with reasoning and tool calls, recovered files and a loss audit), then continues the work. It can also export the current session as a handoff for another agent or teammate, or for Jira, Salesforce, Zendesk or Slack. Use when the user pastes a chatgpt.com/share link, asks to continue work started in ChatGPT, Claude or another assistant, asks to hand off, export or package this session, or asks what was lost when moving a conversation.
---

# Baton: hand off AI work, not just chat text

Baton turns a conversation into a **bundle**: a folder an agent or a person can pick up and continue.

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `HANDOFF.md` | **Start here.** Goal, every user prompt verbatim, where it stopped, open threads, reasoning trail, file status, losses |
| `PROMPT.md` | One paste-ready block for chat apps that cannot read folders |
| `TRANSCRIPT.md` | Full chronological record. Reasoning, tool calls and outputs are folded. |
| `LOSS_AUDIT.md` | What the source did **not** include (redacted tool outputs, blank code, undownloadable files) |
| `files/` | Files rebuilt from the code that created them, each **recovered**, **stale** or **lost** |
| `replay/` | The source's code cells, to regenerate PDFs, ZIPs and similar outputs in a sandbox (never run automatically) |
| `bundle.json` | Canonical machine-readable record with a SHA-256 hash chain |
| `SKILL.md` / `AGENTS.md` | Make the bundle itself a "continue this work" skill / agent context |

## Running it

The CLI ships inside this skill. In the commands below, `BATON` means
`node <this skill's base directory>/scripts/baton.mjs` (Node 18 or newer, no install needed).

## Import and continue (the common case)

1. Run the import. The source can be a share URL, a saved `.html` page, an export `.zip` / `conversations.json`, or a `.jsonl` session:
   ```bash
   BATON import "<source>" -o handoffs/<short-name>
   ```
   Add `--pick "<title words>"` for account exports with many conversations.
2. Read `handoffs/<short-name>/HANDOFF.md` in full. Open `TRANSCRIPT.md` only for the turns you need (search it with Grep).
3. Tell the user in 3–5 lines: the goal, where the work stopped, how many files were recovered, stale or lost, and the high-severity losses.
   **Never claim a lost file exists.**
4. Ask whether to continue from the latest instruction. If yes, copy `files/` into the workspace and carry on.
   Rebuild **lost** files from their descriptions in the transcript, and check **stale** files before relying on them.
5. When a chunk of work is done, write `handoffs/<short-name>/STATE.md` (goal, decisions, next steps).

If fetching a share URL fails (login wall, rate limit, or a private link), ask the user to open the link, save the page as
"Webpage, HTML only", and give you the file path.

## Hand off this session

```bash
BATON session latest -o handoffs/<short-name>            # this project's newest Claude Code session
BATON session latest -o handoffs/<short-name> --zip      # also a .zip to send to someone
BATON session latest --install-skill                     # also install as .claude/skills/continue-<title>/
```
Then show the user `HANDOFF.md` §3–§7 and the bundle path. Mention `PROMPT.md` for pasting into another chat app.

## File it in a business system

```bash
BATON sink handoffs/<name> --to jira --jira-project OPS        # prints the payload (dry run)
BATON sink handoffs/<name> --to salesforce --post              # sends it; needs SALESFORCE_INSTANCE_URL + SALESFORCE_ACCESS_TOKEN
```
Systems: `salesforce` (Case + note + tasks, or `--sf-parent <recordId>`), `jira` (issue in Atlassian Document Format),
`zendesk` (internal-note ticket), `slack` (Block Kit to `SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL`). **Only use `--post` when the user asks you to send it.**

## Check and inspect

```bash
BATON inspect "<source>"        # dry run: what would transfer, what would not
BATON verify handoffs/<name>    # recompute the hash chain; exit 2 if anything was edited
```

## Rules

- Secrets and personal data are redacted by default. Use `--no-redact` only if the user explicitly asks.
- Private context (system prompts, memory, harness notes) is excluded unless `--include-hidden` is passed.
- Never run `replay/` cells without the user's approval. They are third-party code from another AI's sandbox.
- Fetch only the URL the user gave you, one page per request.

#!/usr/bin/env node
// baton: carry AI work between tools, people and systems.

import { cpSync, existsSync, mkdirSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { verifyBundle } from './core/integrity.ts';
import type { BatonBundle } from './core/schema.ts';
import { VERSION } from './core/schema.ts';
import { detectFormat } from './parsers/detect.ts';
import { convert, type ConvertedBundle } from './pipeline.ts';
import { skillName } from './render/handoff.ts';
import { buildSink, SINKS, type SinkName } from './sinks/index.ts';
import { findSession, loadInput, readBundleFiles, writeFiles, writeZip } from './node/io.ts';
import { postSink } from './node/post.ts';
import { startMcpServer } from './mcp/server.ts';

const HELP = `baton ${VERSION}: hand off AI work between tools, not just chat text

USAGE
  baton import <source> [options]     Convert a conversation into a handoff bundle
  baton session [latest|<id>]         Bundle a Claude Code session from this project
  baton inspect <source>              Show what would transfer (and what would not), write nothing
  baton verify <bundle>               Recompute the integrity hash chain of a bundle dir / zip
  baton sink <bundle> --to <system>   Build (and optionally --post) a Salesforce/Jira/Zendesk/Slack payload
  baton mcp                           Run as an MCP server over stdio

SOURCES
  https://chatgpt.com/share/<id>      public ChatGPT share link (fetched once, no login)
  share.html                          a ChatGPT share page saved from the browser
  export.zip | conversations.json     ChatGPT or Claude account data export
  session.jsonl | <dir>               Claude Code session log (dir → newest .jsonl)
  messages.json                       [{role, content}] chat log

OPTIONS
  -o, --out <dir>          output directory (default: ./handoffs/<slug>)
  --zip                    also write <out>.zip
  --pick <id|text>         choose conversation(s) from a multi-conversation export
  --sink <a,b>             add sinks/<system>.json payloads (${SINKS.join(', ')})
  --install-skill          also copy the bundle into .claude/skills/<continue-…>/ for Claude Code
  --no-redact              keep emails, keys and tokens (default: redact)
  --include-hidden         keep private context (system prompts, memory, harness notes)
  --keep-source            store the raw input under source/
  --json                   machine-readable output
  --post                   (sink) send the payload using credentials from env
  --jira-project <KEY>     (sink) Jira project key
  --sf-parent <recordId>   (sink) attach to an existing Salesforce record

Docs: docs/SPEC.md · https://github.com/nimb-ou/App`;

interface Args {
  _: string[];
  flags: Record<string, string | boolean>;
}

function parseArgs(argv: string[]): Args {
  const out: Args = { _: [], flags: {} };
  const valued = new Set(['o', 'out', 'pick', 'sink', 'to', 'jira-project', 'sf-parent', 'now']);
  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
    const a = argv[i];
    if (a === '--') {
      out._.push(...argv.slice(i + 1));
      break;
    }
    const m = a.match(/^--?([^=]+)(?:=(.*))?$/);
    if (m && a !== '-') {
      const key = m[1];
      if (m[2] !== undefined) out.flags[key] = m[2];
      else if (valued.has(key) && i + 1 < argv.length) out.flags[key] = argv[++i];
      else out.flags[key] = true;
    } else out._.push(a);
  }
  return out;
}

const str = (v: string | boolean | undefined): string | undefined => (typeof v === 'string' ? v : undefined);

function summarize(c: ConvertedBundle, outDir?: string): string {
  const b = c.bundle;
  const s = b.stats;
  const lines = [
    `✓ ${b.title}`,
    `  source   ${b.source.tool} (${b.source.kind})${b.source.url ? ` ${b.source.url}` : ''}`,
    `  content  ${s.userMessages} prompts · ${s.assistantMessages} replies · ${s.reasoningBlocks} reasoning · ${s.toolCalls} tool calls`,
    `  files    ${s.artifacts.recovered} recovered · ${s.artifacts.stale} stale · ${s.artifacts.lost} lost${s.artifacts.attached ? ` · ${s.artifacts.attached} attached` : ''}`,
  ];
  const high = b.loss.filter((l) => l.severity === 'high');
  if (high.length) lines.push(`  loss     ${high.map((l) => `${l.count} ${l.category}`).join(' · ')} (see LOSS_AUDIT.md)`);
  const red = Object.values(b.redaction.counts).reduce((x, y) => x + y, 0);
  if (red) lines.push(`  redacted ${red} secret/PII match(es)`);
  lines.push(`  root     ${b.integrity.root}`);
  if (outDir) lines.push(`  → ${outDir}/HANDOFF.md  (start here)`);
  return lines.join('\n');
}

async function cmdImport(source: string | undefined, a: Args, fromSession = false): Promise<void> {
  if (!source) throw new Error('Missing <source>. Run `baton help`.');
  const input = fromSession ? await loadInput(findSession(source)) : await loadInput(source);
  const sinks = (str(a.flags.sink) ?? '').split(',').filter(Boolean) as SinkName[];
  for (const s of sinks) if (!SINKS.includes(s)) throw new Error(`Unknown sink "${s}". Choose from ${SINKS.join(', ')}`);
  const results = convert(input.text, input.filename, {
    url: input.url,
    pick: str(a.flags.pick),
    redact: a.flags['no-redact'] !== true,
    includeHidden: a.flags['include-hidden'] === true,
    keepSource: a.flags['keep-source'] === true,
    sinks,
    sinkOptions: { jiraProject: str(a.flags['jira-project']), salesforceParentId: str(a.flags['sf-parent']) },
    now: str(a.flags.now),
  });
  const base = str(a.flags.o) ?? str(a.flags.out);
  const report: unknown[] = [];
  for (const r of results) {
    const outDir = results.length === 1 && base ? base : join(base ?? 'handoffs', r.slug);
    writeFiles(outDir, r.files);
    if (a.flags.zip) writeZip(`${outDir.replace(/\/$/, '')}.zip`, r.files, r.slug);
    let skillDir: string | undefined;
    if (a.flags['install-skill']) {
      skillDir = join('.claude', 'skills', skillName(r.bundle));
      mkdirSync(skillDir, { recursive: true });
      cpSync(outDir, skillDir, { recursive: true });
    }
    if (a.flags.json) report.push({ out: resolve(outDir), skillDir, id: r.bundle.id, title: r.bundle.title, stats: r.bundle.stats, loss: r.bundle.loss, root: r.bundle.integrity.root });
    else {
      console.log(summarize(r, outDir));
      if (skillDir) console.log(`  skill    installed at ${skillDir}/SKILL.md`);
    }
  }
  if (a.flags.json) console.log(JSON.stringify(report.length === 1 ? report[0] : report, null, 2));
  else if (results.length > 1) console.log(`\n${results.length} conversations written under ${base ?? 'handoffs'}/`);
}

async function cmdInspect(source: string | undefined, a: Args): Promise<void> {
  if (!source) throw new Error('Missing <source>');
  const input = await loadInput(source);
  const fmt = detectFormat(input.text, input.filename);
  const results = convert(input.text, input.filename, { url: input.url, pick: str(a.flags.pick), html: false });
  if (a.flags.json) {
    console.log(JSON.stringify({ format: fmt, conversations: results.map((r) => ({ id: r.bundle.id, title: r.bundle.title, stats: r.bundle.stats, loss: r.bundle.loss, artifacts: r.bundle.artifacts.map(({ content: _c, ...rest }) => rest) })) }, null, 2));
    return;
  }
  console.log(`format: ${fmt.kind} (${fmt.reason})\n`);
  for (const r of results) {
    console.log(summarize(r));
    for (const l of r.bundle.loss) console.log(`    [${l.severity}] ${l.category} ×${l.count}: ${l.detail}`);
    console.log('');
  }
}

function loadBundle(path: string): { bundle: BatonBundle; files: Record<string, string> } {
  const raw = readBundleFiles(path);
  if (!raw['bundle.json']) throw new Error(`${path}: no bundle.json found`);
  const bundle = JSON.parse(raw['bundle.json']) as BatonBundle;
  const files: Record<string, string> = {};
  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(raw)) if (k.startsWith('files/')) files[k.slice(6)] = v;
  return { bundle, files };
}

function cmdVerify(path: string | undefined, a: Args): number {
  if (!path) throw new Error('Missing <bundle>');
  const { bundle, files } = loadBundle(path);
  const r = verifyBundle(bundle, files);
  if (a.flags.json) console.log(JSON.stringify(r, null, 2));
  else if (r.ok) console.log(`✓ intact: ${bundle.events.length} events, ${bundle.artifacts.length} artifacts, root ${r.root}`);
  else {
    console.log(`✗ MODIFIED: expected root ${r.expectedRoot}\n            computed root ${r.root}`);
    if (r.firstBadEvent) console.log(`  first altered event: ${r.firstBadEvent}`);
    if (r.badArtifacts.length) console.log(`  altered files: ${r.badArtifacts.join(', ')}`);
  }
  return r.ok ? 0 : 2;
}

async function cmdSink(path: string | undefined, a: Args): Promise<void> {
  if (!path) throw new Error('Missing <bundle>');
  const to = str(a.flags.to) as SinkName | undefined;
  if (!to || !SINKS.includes(to)) throw new Error(`--to must be one of ${SINKS.join(', ')}`);
  const { bundle } = loadBundle(path);
  const payload = buildSink(to, bundle, { jiraProject: str(a.flags['jira-project']), salesforceParentId: str(a.flags['sf-parent']) });
  if (!a.flags.post) {
    console.log(JSON.stringify(payload, null, 2));
    return;
  }
  const r = await postSink(payload);
  console.log(`${r.ok ? '✓' : '✗'} ${to}: HTTP ${r.status} ${r.url}\n${r.response}`);
  if (!r.ok) process.exitCode = 1;
}

export async function main(argv: string[]): Promise<number> {
  const a = parseArgs(argv);
  const [cmd, arg] = a._;
  if (a.flags.version || a.flags.v) {
    console.log(VERSION);
    return 0;
  }
  switch (cmd) {
    case 'import':
      await cmdImport(arg, a);
      return 0;
    case 'session':
      await cmdImport(arg ?? 'latest', a, true);
      return 0;
    case 'inspect':
      await cmdInspect(arg, a);
      return 0;
    case 'verify':
      return cmdVerify(arg, a);
    case 'sink':
      await cmdSink(arg, a);
      return process.exitCode === 1 ? 1 : 0;
    case 'mcp':
      await startMcpServer();
      return 0;
    case undefined:
    case 'help':
      console.log(HELP);
      return 0;
    default:
      // `baton <url-or-file>` is shorthand for import
      if (/^https?:\/\//.test(cmd) || existsSync(cmd)) {
        await cmdImport(cmd, a);
        return 0;
      }
      console.error(`Unknown command: ${cmd}\n`);
      console.log(HELP);
      return 1;
  }
}

const invokedDirectly = process.argv[1] && /(?:cli\.(?:ts|js)|baton)$/.test(process.argv[1]);
if (invokedDirectly) {
  main(process.argv.slice(2)).then(
    (code) => {
      if (code) process.exitCode = code;
    },
    (err: unknown) => {
      console.error(`baton: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`);
      process.exitCode = 1;
    },
  );
}
recovered · src/core/build.ts · 8.2 KB
// ParsedConversation → BatonBundle: filter private context, redact, recover files, audit losses, hash.

import { recoverArtifacts } from '../recover/artifacts.ts';
import { chainEvents } from './integrity.ts';
import { Redactor } from './redact.ts';
import type { ArtifactStatus, BatonArtifact, BatonBundle, BatonEvent, BuildOptions, BundleStats, LossItem, ParsedConversation } from './schema.ts';
import { SCHEMA_ID, VERSION } from './schema.ts';
import { sha256Hex } from './sha256.ts';
import { extractUrls, isRecord } from './util.ts';
import type { FileMap } from './zip.ts';

export interface BuildResult {
  bundle: BatonBundle;
  /** Extra files produced alongside the bundle (e.g. replay kit). */
  extraFiles: FileMap;
}

const NOISE_URL = /^https?:\/\/(?:images\.openai\.com|cdn\.oaistatic\.com|files\.oaiusercontent\.com)\//;

export function buildBundle(parsed: ParsedConversation, options: BuildOptions = {}): BuildResult {
  const redact = options.redact !== false;
  const includeHidden = options.includeHidden === true;
  const redactor = new Redactor(redact);

  let privateDropped = 0;
  const events: BatonEvent[] = [];
  for (const draft of parsed.events) {
    const isPrivate = isRecord(draft.meta) && draft.meta.private === true;
    if (isPrivate && !includeHidden) {
      privateDropped++;
      continue;
    }
    const e: BatonEvent = { ...draft, seq: events.length + 1, id: draft.id || `e${events.length + 1}` };
    e.text = redactor.apply(e.text);
    if (e.tool) {
      e.tool = { ...e.tool };
      if (e.tool.input) e.tool.input = redactor.apply(e.tool.input);
      if (e.tool.args) e.tool.args = redactArgs(e.tool.args, redactor);
    }
    events.push(e);
  }

  // Recover files from the (already redacted) tool calls.
  const { artifacts, replay } = recoverArtifacts(events, parsed.source.kind, parsed.source.cwd);
  for (const att of parsed.attachments ?? []) {
    const content = redactor.apply(att.content);
    artifacts.push({ path: att.path, originalPath: att.path, status: 'attached', content, bytes: new TextEncoder().encode(content).length, sha256: sha256Hex(content), note: att.note });
  }
  artifacts.sort((a, b) => a.path.localeCompare(b.path));

  const links = collectLinks(events, parsed.sourceLinks ?? []);
  const stats = computeStats(events, artifacts);
  const loss = auditLoss(parsed, events, artifacts, privateDropped, redactor.counts, Boolean(replay));

  const now = options.now ?? new Date().toISOString();
  const idSeed = [parsed.source.kind, parsed.source.conversationId ?? parsed.source.url ?? '', parsed.title, events[0]?.text ?? ''].join('\u0000');
  const root = chainEvents(events, artifacts);

  const bundle: BatonBundle = {
    schema: SCHEMA_ID,
    id: sha256Hex(idSeed).slice(0, 16),
    title: redactor.apply(parsed.title),
    source: parsed.source,
    events,
    artifacts,
    loss,
    links,
    redaction: { enabled: redact, counts: { ...redactor.counts } },
    integrity: { algorithm: 'sha256-chain', root, count: events.length },
    stats,
    generator: { name: 'baton', version: VERSION, generatedAt: now },
  };
  return { bundle, extraFiles: replay ?? {} };
}

function redactArgs(args: Record<string, unknown>, r: Redactor): Record<string, unknown> {
  const out: Record<string, unknown> = {};
  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(args)) {
    if (typeof v === 'string') out[k] = r.apply(v);
    else if (Array.isArray(v)) out[k] = v.map((x) => (isRecord(x) ? redactArgs(x, r) : typeof x === 'string' ? r.apply(x) : x));
    else if (isRecord(v)) out[k] = redactArgs(v, r);
    else out[k] = v;
  }
  return out;
}

function collectLinks(events: BatonEvent[], sourceLinks: string[]): string[] {
  const set = new Set<string>();
  for (const e of events) if (e.kind === 'message' || e.kind === 'reasoning') for (const u of extractUrls(e.text)) set.add(u);
  for (const u of sourceLinks) set.add(u);
  return [...set].filter((u) => !NOISE_URL.test(u) && !u.includes('{')).sort();
}

function computeStats(events: BatonEvent[], artifacts: BatonArtifact[]): BundleStats {
  const count = (f: (e: BatonEvent) => boolean) => events.filter(f).length;
  const byStatus: Record<ArtifactStatus, number> = { recovered: 0, stale: 0, lost: 0, attached: 0 };
  for (const a of artifacts) byStatus[a.status]++;
  const times = events.map((e) => (e.time ? Date.parse(e.time) : NaN)).filter((t) => Number.isFinite(t));
  return {
    userMessages: count((e) => e.kind === 'message' && e.role === 'user'),
    assistantMessages: count((e) => e.kind === 'message' && e.role === 'assistant'),
    reasoningBlocks: count((e) => e.kind === 'reasoning'),
    toolCalls: count((e) => e.kind === 'tool_call'),
    toolResults: count((e) => e.kind === 'tool_result'),
    hiddenEvents: count((e) => e.hidden === true),
    redactedBySource: count((e) => e.redactedBySource === true),
    artifacts: byStatus,
    durationSeconds: times.length > 1 ? Math.round((Math.max(...times) - Math.min(...times)) / 1000) : undefined,
  };
}

export function auditLoss(
  parsed: ParsedConversation,
  events: BatonEvent[],
  artifacts: BatonArtifact[],
  privateDropped: number,
  redactions: Record<string, number>,
  hasReplay: boolean,
): LossItem[] {
  const items: LossItem[] = [];
  const group = (pred: (e: BatonEvent) => boolean) => {
    const m = new Map<string, number>();
    for (const e of events) if (pred(e)) m.set(e.tool?.name ?? 'tool', (m.get(e.tool?.name ?? 'tool') ?? 0) + 1);
    return m;
  };

  const outs = group((e) => e.kind === 'tool_result' && e.redactedBySource === true);
  const outTotal = [...outs.values()].reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
  if (outTotal) {
    items.push({ category: 'tool-outputs', count: outTotal, severity: 'high', detail: `The source replaced ${outTotal} tool output(s) with a placeholder (${fmtGroup(outs)}). Results of searches, code runs and connector calls are not available. The receiving agent must re-run them if needed.` });
  }
  const ins = group((e) => e.kind === 'tool_call' && e.redactedBySource === true);
  const inTotal = [...ins.values()].reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
  if (inTotal) {
    items.push({ category: 'tool-inputs', count: inTotal, severity: 'high', detail: `${inTotal} tool call(s) have no input in the source (${fmtGroup(ins)}). The code or arguments they ran cannot be recovered.` });
  }
  const lost = artifacts.filter((a) => a.status === 'lost');
  if (lost.length) {
    items.push({ category: 'files-lost', count: lost.length, severity: 'high', detail: `${lost.length} file(s) were created or linked but their contents are not in the source: ${lost.slice(0, 8).map((a) => a.path).join(', ')}${lost.length > 8 ? ', …' : ''}.${hasReplay ? ' Some derived outputs can be regenerated with replay/.' : ''}` });
  }
  const stale = artifacts.filter((a) => a.status === 'stale');
  if (stale.length) {
    items.push({ category: 'files-stale', count: stale.length, severity: 'warn', detail: `${stale.length} file(s) were recovered exactly as last written, but later code changed them in ways that cannot be replayed statically: ${stale.slice(0, 8).map((a) => a.path).join(', ')}${stale.length > 8 ? ', …' : ''}.` });
  }
  const recovered = artifacts.filter((a) => a.status === 'recovered').length;
  if (recovered) items.push({ category: 'files-recovered', count: recovered, severity: 'info', detail: `${recovered} file(s) were reconstructed byte-for-byte from the code that created them.` });
  items.push(...(parsed.loss ?? []));
  if (privateDropped) items.push({ category: 'private-context', count: privateDropped, severity: 'info', detail: `${privateDropped} private context item(s) (system prompts, memory, custom instructions, harness metadata) were excluded. Use --include-hidden to keep them.` });
  const redTotal = Object.values(redactions).reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
  if (redTotal) items.push({ category: 'baton-redactions', count: redTotal, severity: 'info', detail: `Baton redacted ${redTotal} secret/PII match(es) before export (${Object.entries(redactions).map(([k, v]) => `${k}: ${v}`).join(', ')}). Use --no-redact to disable.` });

  const order = { high: 0, warn: 1, info: 2 } as const;
  return items.sort((a, b) => order[a.severity] - order[b.severity]);
}

function fmtGroup(m: Map<string, number>): string {
  return [...m.entries()].sort((a, b) => b[1] - a[1]).map(([k, v]) => `${k} ×${v}`).join(', ');
}
recovered · src/core/integrity.ts · 2.3 KB
// Tamper-evidence: a SHA-256 hash chain over normalised events and artifacts.
// h_0 = sha256(schema id); h_i = sha256(h_{i-1} + stableJSON(event_i without hash)).
// The root also commits to every artifact's content hash, so edited files are detected too.

import type { BatonArtifact, BatonBundle, BatonEvent } from './schema.ts';
import { SCHEMA_ID } from './schema.ts';
import { sha256Hex } from './sha256.ts';
import { stableStringify } from './util.ts';

function eventPayload(e: BatonEvent): string {
  const { hash: _ignored, ...rest } = e;
  return stableStringify(rest);
}

function artifactPayload(a: BatonArtifact): string {
  return stableStringify({ path: a.path, status: a.status, sha256: a.sha256 ?? null });
}

export function chainEvents(events: BatonEvent[], artifacts: BatonArtifact[]): string {
  let h = sha256Hex(SCHEMA_ID);
  for (const e of events) {
    h = sha256Hex(h + eventPayload(e));
    e.hash = h;
  }
  for (const a of [...artifacts].sort((x, y) => x.path.localeCompare(y.path))) {
    h = sha256Hex(h + artifactPayload(a));
  }
  return h;
}

export interface VerifyResult {
  ok: boolean;
  root: string;
  expectedRoot: string;
  firstBadEvent?: string;
  badArtifacts: string[];
}

/** Recompute the chain from a bundle (and optionally the shipped file contents) and compare. */
export function verifyBundle(bundle: BatonBundle, files?: Record<string, string>): VerifyResult {
  let h = sha256Hex(SCHEMA_ID);
  let firstBad: string | undefined;
  for (const e of bundle.events) {
    h = sha256Hex(h + eventPayload(e));
    if (!firstBad && e.hash && e.hash !== h) firstBad = e.id;
  }
  const badArtifacts: string[] = [];
  for (const a of [...bundle.artifacts].sort((x, y) => x.path.localeCompare(y.path))) {
    // Hash what is actually shipped: the file on disk if present, else the content embedded in bundle.json.
    const shipped = files?.[a.path] ?? a.content;
    const actual = shipped !== undefined ? sha256Hex(shipped) : a.sha256;
    if (a.sha256 && actual !== a.sha256) badArtifacts.push(a.path);
    h = sha256Hex(h + artifactPayload({ ...a, sha256: actual }));
  }
  return {
    ok: h === bundle.integrity.root && !firstBad && badArtifacts.length === 0,
    root: h,
    expectedRoot: bundle.integrity.root,
    firstBadEvent: firstBad,
    badArtifacts,
  };
}
stale · src/core/redact.ts · 3.2 KB · rewritten by Python open(…, "w")
// Secret and PII redaction applied before anything leaves the user's machine.
// Conservative by design: false positives cost a little readability; false negatives leak keys.

export interface RedactionRule {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  re: RegExp;
}

export const DEFAULT_RULES: RedactionRule[] = [
  { id: 'private_key', label: 'PRIVATE KEY', re: /-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----[\s\S]*?-----END [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----/g },
  { id: 'anthropic_key', label: 'ANTHROPIC KEY', re: /\bsk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}/g },
  { id: 'openai_key', label: 'OPENAI KEY', re: /\bsk-(?:proj-|svcacct-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}/g },
  { id: 'github_token', label: 'GITHUB TOKEN', re: /\b(?:gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{30,}|github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{40,})/g },
  { id: 'aws_access_key', label: 'AWS KEY', re: /\b(?:AKIA|ASIA)[0-9A-Z]{16}\b/g },
  { id: 'google_api_key', label: 'GOOGLE KEY', re: /\bAIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}\b/g },
  { id: 'slack_token', label: 'SLACK TOKEN', re: /\bxox[abprs]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}/g },
  { id: 'stripe_key', label: 'STRIPE KEY', re: /\b(?:sk|rk)_(?:live|test)_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}/g },
  { id: 'jwt', label: 'JWT', re: /\beyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}/g },
  { id: 'bearer', label: 'BEARER TOKEN', re: /\b(Bearer\s+)[A-Za-z0-9._~+/-]{20,}=*/g },
  { id: 'password_assign', label: 'PASSWORD', re: /\b((?:password|passwd|pwd|secret|api[_-]?key|access[_-]?token)\s*[:=]\s*)(["']?)[^\s"']{6,}\2/gi },
  { id: 'email', label: 'EMAIL', re: /\b[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}\b/g },
  { id: 'phone', label: 'PHONE', re: /(?<![\w.])\+?\d{1,3}[\s.-]?\(?\d{2,4}\)?[\s.-]\d{3,4}[\s.-]\d{3,4}(?![\w.])/g },
  { id: 'card', label: 'CARD', re: /\b(?:\d[ -]?){13,19}\b/g },
];

// Addresses that are part of software (not people) and are safe to keep.
const EMAIL_ALLOW = /^(?:noreply|no-reply|donotreply|example|user|test)@|@(?:example\.(?:com|org|net)|anthropic\.com|users\.noreply\.github\.com)$/i;

export class Redactor {
  counts: Record<string, number> = {};
  rules: RedactionRule[];
  enabled: boolean;

  constructor(enabled = true, rules: RedactionRule[] = DEFAULT_RULES) {
    this.enabled = enabled;
    this.rules = rules;
  }

  apply(text: string): string {
    if (!this.enabled || !text) return text;
    let out = text;
    for (const rule of this.rules) {
      out = out.replace(rule.re, (match: string, ...groups: unknown[]) => {
        if (rule.id === 'email' && EMAIL_ALLOW.test(match)) return match;
        if (rule.id === 'card' && !luhn(match)) return match;
        if (rule.id === 'phone' && match.replace(/\D/g, '').length < 9) return match;
        this.counts[rule.id] = (this.counts[rule.id] ?? 0) + 1;
        const prefix = (rule.id === 'bearer' || rule.id === 'password_assign') && typeof groups[0] === 'string' ? groups[0] : '';
        return `${prefix}[REDACTED:${rule.label}]`;
      });
    }
    return out;
  }
}

function luhn(s: string): boolean {
  const digits = s.replace(/\D/g, '');
  if (digits.length < 13 || digits.length > 19) return false;
  let sum = 0;
  let dbl = false;
  for (let i = digits.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    let d = digits.charCodeAt(i) - 48;
    if (dbl) {
      d *= 2;
      if (d > 9) d -= 9;
    }
    sum += d;
    dbl = !dbl;
  }
  return sum % 10 === 0;
}

// Baton bundle format: the canonical, vendor-neutral representation of a piece of AI work.
// Spec: docs/SPEC.md. Keep this file free of runtime code other than constants.

export const SCHEMA_ID = 'baton.bundle/v1';
export const VERSION = '0.1.0';

export type Role = 'user' | 'assistant' | 'system' | 'tool';

export type EventKind =
  | 'message' // prose from user or assistant
  | 'reasoning' // reasoning summary / thinking block
  | 'tool_call' // code execution, search, connector call
  | 'tool_result' // output of a tool call
  | 'attachment' // file or image attached to a message
  | 'note'; // structural marker (e.g. "worked for 24m")

export type SourceKind =
  | 'chatgpt-share'
  | 'chatgpt-export'
  | 'claude-export'
  | 'claude-code'
  | 'openai-messages'
  | 'unknown';

export interface ToolInfo {
  name: string;
  title?: string; // human label, e.g. "Searching OpenAlex…"
  language?: string;
  input?: string; // code / query / JSON args, as displayed
  args?: Record<string, unknown>; // structured args for file-editing tools (Write/Edit/MultiEdit)
  callId?: string;
}

export interface BatonEvent {
  id: string;
  seq: number;
  kind: EventKind;
  role: Role;
  time?: string; // ISO-8601
  text: string; // Markdown
  tool?: ToolInfo;
  model?: string;
  hidden?: boolean; // hidden in the source UI (system, memory, commentary)
  redactedBySource?: boolean; // the source replaced the content with a placeholder
  meta?: Record<string, unknown>;
  hash?: string; // chain hash, filled by integrity.ts
}

export type ArtifactStatus =
  | 'recovered' // full content reconstructed exactly from source
  | 'stale' // reconstructed, but later modified by code we could not replay statically
  | 'lost' // referenced/created in source, content unavailable
  | 'attached'; // content shipped with the source itself (e.g. Claude export attachment)

export interface BatonArtifact {
  path: string; // relative path inside bundle files/ (POSIX)
  originalPath: string;
  status: ArtifactStatus;
  content?: string;
  bytes?: number;
  sha256?: string;
  versions?: number;
  recoveredFrom?: string[]; // event ids
  modifiedBy?: string[]; // event ids that touched it after the last full write
  note?: string;
}

export type Severity = 'info' | 'warn' | 'high';

export interface LossItem {
  category: string;
  count: number;
  severity: Severity;
  detail: string;
}

export interface BatonSource {
  kind: SourceKind;
  tool: string; // human name: "ChatGPT", "Claude", "Claude Code"
  url?: string;
  conversationId?: string;
  createdAt?: string;
  updatedAt?: string;
  model?: string;
  cwd?: string;
  gitBranch?: string;
}

export interface BatonBundle {
  schema: typeof SCHEMA_ID;
  id: string;
  title: string;
  source: BatonSource;
  events: BatonEvent[];
  artifacts: BatonArtifact[];
  loss: LossItem[];
  links: string[];
  redaction: { enabled: boolean; counts: Record<string, number> };
  integrity: { algorithm: 'sha256-chain'; root: string; count: number };
  stats: BundleStats;
  generator: { name: 'baton'; version: string; generatedAt: string };
}

export interface BundleStats {
  userMessages: number;
  assistantMessages: number;
  reasoningBlocks: number;
  toolCalls: number;
  toolResults: number;
  hiddenEvents: number;
  redactedBySource: number;
  artifacts: Record<ArtifactStatus, number>;
  durationSeconds?: number;
}

/** Output of a parser, before normalisation/redaction/recovery. */
export interface ParsedConversation {
  title: string;
  source: BatonSource;
  events: Omit<BatonEvent, 'seq' | 'hash'>[];
  /** Files that came with the source (attachments with extracted text). */
  attachments?: { path: string; content: string; note?: string }[];
  /** URLs the source itself lists (e.g. ChatGPT safe_urls). */
  sourceLinks?: string[];
  /** Parser-level loss observations (things only the parser can know). */
  loss?: LossItem[];
}

export interface BuildOptions {
  redact?: boolean; // default true
  includeHidden?: boolean; // default false
  now?: string; // ISO timestamp override for reproducible output
}
